I tried making lavender-honey tea once, but I used culinary lavender instead of the kind meant for steeping, and it tasted like I was drinking perfume. It was awful. I ended up pouring the whole pot down the sink, and then my kitchen smelled like a soap factory for three days. Which I guess isn't the worst thing to smell like. Better than that time the garbage disposal broke and my apartment smelled like old broccoli. I had to call the super, and he took forever to fix it because he was busy watching some big soccer match. I don't really get soccer, honestly. There's just so much running back and forth and hardly any scoring. It's kind of like watching a really intense game of ping-pong but on a giant field. \n\nBut, right, my daily life. Um, yeah, I'm getting through the basic errands and stuff, but I'm definitely less reliable with my volunteer work. Like the fair planning and the design follow-through I usually do. People usually count on me for that, and I've just been kind of... dropping the ball lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your current state is making it harder for you to concentrate on things?\n\nPatient: Um, concentrating? No, I don't think so. I haven't really noticed any issues with that. I mean, I can still focus on things just fine. \n\nLike yesterday, I sat down and read this entire article about the history of the Ferris wheel. It was actually super fascinating. Did you know the first one was built for the 1893 World's Fair in Chicago? It was supposed to rival the Eiffel Tower, which is wild to think about. I've always wanted to go to Paris and see the Eiffel Tower, but I've heard the lines are just horrendous. My friend Sarah went last year, and she said she waited for three hours just to get to the second level. And then it started raining, and she didn't have an umbrella. Which reminds me, I still need to buy a new umbrella. I was thinking about getting one of those clear bubble ones. They look so cute, like you're in a little personal greenhouse. Though I guess if it's sunny, you'd literally bake in there like a tomato. I tried growing tomatoes on my balcony once, but the pigeons kept eating them before they even turned red. Pigeons are so bold in the city, aren't they? They just walk right up to you like they own the sidewalk. \n\nBut yeah, no, my concentration is fine. I haven't had any trouble focusing on things.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about when you first started feeling this way and if it's been consistent over the past two months?\n\nPatient: Um, when it started? Yeah, it's been about two months now. It pretty much started right when I decided I needed to completely overhaul my daily routine and how I present myself to the world. I just felt like I needed a fresh start, you know? \n\nI bought all these new planners—the really nice ones with the thick paper where the ink doesn't bleed through. I even got a set of those pastel highlighters because I saw this video of a girl color-coding her entire life, and it just looked so calming. Her desk was perfectly organized, and she had this little succulent in a geometric pot. I tried to keep a succulent once, but I think I overwatered it because it just turned to mush. Everyone says they're the easiest plants to keep alive, but I swear ferns are easier. My grandmother had this massive Boston fern on her porch that she basically ignored, and it thrived. She used to sit out there and knit these really intricate lace doilies. I don't think people use doilies much anymore, do they? They feel very vintage. Kind of like those hard ribbon candies they sell around the holidays. The ones that look pretty but taste like dusty peppermint and always get stuck together in a giant clump in the bowl. \n\nBut, um, yeah, it's been pretty consistent for the last two months, ever since I tried changing up my routine and everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to find pleasure in things you used to enjoy, even if you're technically still doing them?\n\nPatient: Um, finding pleasure in things? No, I don't think so. I still enjoy the things I do. Like, I'm still doing my volunteer stuff, even if I'm a little behind on it, and I still like it. \n\nActually, I was just looking at these new fabric swatches for the fair booths yesterday. They have this gorgeous teal color that I think would look amazing against the white tents. Teal is such an underrated color, honestly. It's like blue and green had a perfect baby. I painted my bedroom teal when I was in high school. My dad was so mad because it took like four coats of primer to cover it up when we moved out. We moved to this house that had this huge oak tree in the front yard, and in the fall, it would drop a million acorns. The squirrels were out of control. They would literally sit on the fence and chatter at my dog, Buster. Buster was a golden retriever, and he was the sweetest thing, but he was terrified of the vacuum cleaner. Like, if you even opened the closet where we kept it, he would run and hide under my bed. Which is funny because he wasn't scared of thunder at all. He would just sleep right through these massive thunderstorms. I love a good thunderstorm, as long as I don't have to drive in it. People completely forget how to drive the second a single drop of rain hits the windshield. It's like their brains just short-circuit. \n\nBut yeah, no, I still enjoy things just fine. I haven't noticed any problem with that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any thoughts of self-harm or suicide?\n\nPatient: Um, thoughts of hurting myself? No, definitely not. I haven't had any thoughts like that at all. I mean, I can't even stand the sight of blood, to be honest. \n\nLike, last week I was making a salad and I accidentally nicked my finger with the paring knife. It wasn't even a deep cut, but it bled a little, and I had to sit down on the kitchen floor for like ten minutes because I felt so lightheaded. I used one of those band-aids with the little cartoon dinosaurs on it. My little cousin left a whole box of them at my apartment last time she visited. She's obsessed with the T-Rex right now, which is so funny because when I was her age, I was completely terrified of dinosaurs. I think it was because my older brother made me watch Jurassic Park when I was way too young. That scene with the Jell-O shaking in the spoon? Still gives me the creeps. But Jell-O is such a weird food anyway, right? It's like a liquid that decided to be a solid but couldn't fully commit. My mom used to make this green Jell-O salad for Thanksgiving with shredded carrots and pineapple in it. Everyone always pretended to like it, but I swear she was the only one who actually ate it. Thanksgiving food is so heavy anyway, you know? By the time you get to dessert, you just want to take a nap on the couch. \n\nBut um, no, to answer your question, I haven't had any thoughts of self-harm or anything like that. I'm totally fine in that department.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about your future most of the time over the past two months?\n\nPatient: Um, hopeless? No, I wouldn't say I feel hopeless about the future. I mean, I'm not really sure what the future looks like right now, but I don't feel bad about it. \n\nActually, speaking of the future, I was just looking at these astrology apps the other day. My friend Sarah is super into all that stuff. She made me download this one that tells you exactly where all the planets are at the exact minute you were born. Apparently, my moon is in Taurus, which she says means I like comfortable things and good food. Which is pretty accurate, honestly. I love a good grilled cheese sandwich. But you have to make it with the right bread, you know? Sourdough is the best because it gets that perfect crunch on the outside. I tried making my own sourdough starter during the pandemic, like everyone else did. I named him Doughy, but I completely forgot to feed him for like a week, and he started smelling like old gym socks. It was so gross. I ended up throwing the whole jar away. Glass jars are so useful, though. I save all my pasta sauce jars to put things in, like buttons and paperclips. I have this one jar that's just full of those little plastic tags they put on bread bags. I don't even know why I save them. Maybe I saw a craft project on Pinterest once and thought I'd do it someday? Pinterest is a black hole. You go on there looking for a recipe for chicken parmesan, and three hours later, you're looking at ideas for a rustic woodland wedding, even though you're not even dating anyone. \n\nBut um, yeah, no, I don't feel hopeless about my future or anything. I'm just kind of taking it day by day right now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Um, interfering? I wouldn't say it's significantly interfering, no. I mean, I'm still getting through my basic errands and normal commitments just fine. 

Like, I went to the grocery store yesterday to get oat milk. Have you noticed how many types of milk there are now? It's honestly overwhelming. Almond, oat, soy, macadamia... I tried macadamia nut milk once in my coffee and it just tasted like drinking a candle. Speaking of candles, I bought this huge three-wick one the other day that was supposed to smell like "Autumn Woods," but it just smells exactly like cheap men's cologne. Which isn't a bad smell, I guess, but it's really weird when you're trying to eat a bowl of cereal and your kitchen smells like a middle school dance. Middle school was such an awkward time. I had braces and these terrible bangs that I cut myself with kitchen scissors because I thought I could follow a YouTube tutorial. Spoiler alert, I could not. I looked like a medieval peasant for like six months until they grew out. 

But um, as far as functioning goes, I guess I have been less reliable lately in the volunteer and creative roles that people usually count on me for. Like the fair planning and the design follow-through. I was supposed to send over the final layout for the vendor booths on Tuesday, and I just kind of haven't done it yet. Which reminds me, one of the vendors this year is this lady who makes jewelry out of antique spoons. It's actually really cool. She takes the handles and bends them into rings. I bought one last year that had a little rose pattern stamped on it, but I lost it at the beach. The beach is always so much better in theory than in reality, you know? You think it's going to be so relaxing, but then you just end up with sand in your sandwich and a terrible sunburn on the tops of your feet. Sunburned feet are the absolute worst because you can't even put real shoes on for a week. 

So yeah, I'm getting my basic errands done, just kind of dropping the ball a little on the fair designs and stuff.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n20\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Um, well, I came in because lately I've been trying to clean up my image and my whole routine, you know, for the arts board stuff. Which, by the way, the arts board has been completely chaotic this year. They changed the venue for the spring exhibition like three times, and the natural lighting in the new hall is just atrocious for mixed media pieces, which is what I mostly do. It totally washes out the watercolors. \n\nBut anyway, since I started trying to get my act together for all that, I’ve just been way more tense and down than I expected. Like, I’m still doing the basics every day, but I keep dropping little things I normally really care about. Like my fair planning notes or my design drafts. I used to color-code all my drafts with these specific Japanese gel pens—have you ever tried them? They glide so smoothly, I actually had to special order them online because the local craft store stopped stocking the 0.38 millimeter ones, and the thicker ones just bleed through the sketch paper. \n\nBut yeah... now I just kind of let those notes and drafts slide, and then I feel so guilty about falling behind that I just cancel my plans with people instead of actually catching up on what I missed.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy doing your mixed media art pieces?\n\nPatient: Um, I mean, I guess I still like it in theory? But honestly, I haven't really picked up my brushes much lately. It's just so frustrating with the lighting at the new venue, like I said. Which reminds me, the coordinator for the exhibition, Sarah—she's usually so on top of things, but I think she's just completely overwhelmed this year. She actually texted me the other day at like 11 PM asking if I knew anyone who could do floral arrangements for the reception because her original florist backed out. I told her about this little shop down on 4th Street that did the flowers for my cousin's baby shower. They had these gorgeous blue hydrangeas, just massive blooms, you know? My cousin was so thrilled, she actually cried, though she was pregnant, so I guess she was crying at everything. But yeah, Sarah hasn't even texted me back about the florist, so who knows what they're doing for the reception now. But art... yeah, I just haven't had the energy for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're not enjoying things that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know, I guess I just haven't really noticed? Like, I'm still doing stuff, I just... I don't know if I'm enjoying it less or if I'm just distracted. Like with the fair planning, I usually love organizing that. I have this whole binder system. Oh, actually, I was at the stationary store the other day trying to find new dividers for it, because the plastic ones I bought last year cracked. Can you believe that? They were supposed to be heavy-duty, but they just snapped right at the rings. I ended up talking to the cashier for like twenty minutes about how the quality of office supplies has just tanked recently. My friend Chloe used to work at the library, and she told me they actually send your account to a collection agency if you rack up more than fifty dollars in fines. Can you imagine getting a call from a debt collector over a book about 18th-century weaving techniques? She said it happened to this one guy who checked out like twenty DVDs and just moved out of state. \n\nBut, um, what were we talking about? Oh, anxious. Yeah, I don't know, I guess I just feel more guilty than anxious? Like, guilty about the plans I'm canceling and the notes I'm not writing. But not really anxious all day long.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the day?\n\nPatient: Um, sad most of the day? No, I don't think so. I mean, I definitely feel down sometimes, especially when I'm sitting in my apartment trying to figure out what to do with myself, but it's not like a constant, all-day sadness. It's more just this heavy feeling that comes and goes. \n\nActually, speaking of my apartment, I was trying to rearrange my living room yesterday to maybe make it feel a bit more inspiring, you know? I have this huge Monstera plant—I named him Bruce—and he's just taking over the corner by the window. I tried to move him closer to the bookshelf, but the pot is so incredibly heavy. It's this beautiful terracotta one I found at a flea market last summer. The guy selling it was telling me how it was handmade in Mexico, and he had all these amazing stories about his travels. He even showed me pictures of his dog, this little scruffy terrier mix that rides in the basket of his bicycle. It was so cute. But anyway, I couldn't even lift the pot, so Bruce is just stuck where he is, blocking half the sunlight. \n\nBut yeah, sad? Not really all day. Just... sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Um, lost interest? I don't... I mean, I guess it's kind of like what I was saying before about the fair planning. I'm still doing things, I just... I don't know if the interest is gone or if I'm just not focusing on it. Like, I still *want* to do things, theoretically. \n\nLike baking! I usually love baking on the weekends. I have this sourdough starter that I've kept alive for like, two years now. Her name is Doughlene. But lately, I just look at the jar in the fridge and think about all the flour and the mess and the kneading. Oh, did you know there's this specific type of flour from Italy, 00 flour, that makes the absolute best pizza dough? I ordered a huge bag of it online a few months ago because I was going to host a pizza night for my friends. The shipping cost more than the flour itself because it's so heavy, and the delivery guy looked so mad when he dropped it on my porch. I felt so bad, I actually tried to offer him a bottle of water, but he just grunted and walked away. I mean, I get it, it's hot out, but still. So now I just have this giant bag of fancy flour sitting in my pantry, and I haven't even opened it. \n\nSo, yeah, I don't know if I've *lost* interest, I just... haven't been doing it as much, I guess?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um, weight? No, I haven't really noticed any changes there. I mean, my clothes all still fit exactly the same, so I don't think I've lost anything. \n\nThough, speaking of clothes, I was trying to organize my closet the other day because it's just an absolute disaster zone. I have all these vintage dresses that I bought at thrift stores over the years. There's this one I found in this tiny shop downtown—it's this gorgeous emerald green silk from the 1950s. The woman who owned the shop told me it belonged to her grandmother who used to wear it to jazz clubs. Can you imagine? Just going out to listen to live jazz in a silk dress? I actually tried to find some good jazz clubs around here, but the only one I found had a cover charge of like thirty dollars just to get in the door, and then the drinks were ridiculously expensive on top of that. My friend Liam went once and said he spent fifty bucks just on two cocktails that were mostly ice. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, no weight loss that I've noticed. Everything's pretty much the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that you wouldn't have felt guilty about before?\n\nPatient: Um, guilty? Yeah, actually. I came in because ever since I started trying to clean up my image and my whole routine for this arts board stuff I'm doing, I've been way more tense and down than I expected to be. Like, I'm still doing the basics of my day, but I keep dropping little things I normally care about, like my fair planning notes or my design drafts. And then I feel so guilty about it that I end up canceling plans with people instead of just catching up on the work. \n\nWhich is terrible because I canceled on my friend Sarah last week. We were supposed to go to this new vintage market that popped up near the river. I love those things. You always find the weirdest stuff. Last time I went to one, I found this collection of old postcards from the 1920s, all written in French. I don't even speak French, but the handwriting was so beautiful, all loopy and perfectly slanted. I actually bought a fountain pen after that because I wanted to learn how to write like that, but the ink just got everywhere. I ruined a perfectly good pair of jeans. They were my favorite ones, too, the light wash ones that actually fit my waist without needing a belt. Finding jeans that don't need a belt is basically impossible these days, don't you think? \n\nBut yeah, the guilt. I just feel bad about dropping the ball on the arts board stuff and then hiding from my friends because of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things even when there's no obvious reason to worry?\n\nPatient: Um, worrying for no reason? No, I don't think so. I mean, I stress about the arts board and my projects, obviously, but I don't just sit around worrying about nothing. \n\nWell, except maybe my houseplants. I have this maidenhair fern that is just ridiculously dramatic. If I don't mist it exactly every other day, the leaves get all crispy and drop off. I actually bought this special brass mister for it a few weeks ago because I wanted it to look nice sitting out. But when it arrived, it was totally just cheap plastic painted gold. It even chipped on the bottom the very first time I set it down on my windowsill. Which is super annoying because my windowsill is that nice exposed brick? I live in one of those old converted loft buildings. It looks really cool, but the insulation is just terrible. In the winter, you practically have to wear a winter coat indoors. My friend Liam—the one I mentioned before—he actually brought over this giant heated blanket once because he refused to watch a movie on my couch without it. \n\nBut yeah, anyway... I don't really worry about random things, just the actual stuff I have going on.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um, how long? It's been about two months now. Basically ever since I decided I needed to completely overhaul my routine and the way I present myself for this arts board. \n\nWhich, honestly, overhauling a routine is so much harder than all those lifestyle blogs make it look. I started following this one influencer who wakes up at 5 AM every day to do yoga and drink lemon water. I tried the lemon water thing for like three days, but it made my teeth feel all weird and squeaky? I read somewhere that the acid can ruin your enamel, so I stopped. But then I had this whole bag of lemons sitting in my fridge. I ended up trying to make lemon bars from scratch. They actually turned out pretty good, except I accidentally used a pan that was too big, so they were really thin, almost like lemon crackers. I brought them over to my neighbor's apartment anyway. She's this sweet older lady who has this huge collection of vintage teacups. She actually gave me one once—it has these tiny hand-painted violets all over it. I keep it on my desk now to hold my paperclips, which I know is probably sacrilege for a nice teacup, but it looks really cute. \n\nBut yeah, anyway... about two months of feeling like this. Ever since I started trying to change everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble sleeping at night?\n\nPatient: Um, trouble sleeping? Not really, no. I mean, I sleep fine once I actually get to bed. \n\nGetting to bed is the hard part sometimes because I get so distracted. Like the other night, I was trying to wind down, and I started looking at throw pillows online. My couch is this really dark gray, and it just looks so sad lately. I thought maybe some mustard yellow pillows would brighten it up, you know? But then I went down this huge rabbit hole reading about the psychology of color in interior design. Apparently, yellow can make people feel anxious or even make babies cry more? Which is wild. I don't have a baby, obviously, but still. It made me rethink the whole yellow idea. So then I started looking at sage green instead, which is supposed to be calming. But finding the exact right shade of green is impossible online because everyone's monitor is calibrated differently. I ordered a sweater once that looked forest green on my laptop, but when it showed up, it was practically neon. I couldn't even return it because it was final sale, so now it's just sitting in the back of my closet mocking me. I should probably just donate it to that thrift store down the street. The one with the really grumpy orange cat that sleeps on the cash register. \n\nBut, uh, no, my sleep is fine. I usually sleep through the whole night once I'm actually asleep.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from anything?\n\nPatient: Um, unable to experience pleasure? No, I wouldn't say that. I still enjoy things. \n\nLike, I still really love going to this little bakery near my apartment. They have these amazing almond croissants. I actually went there yesterday morning, and the line was out the door, which is so unusual for a Tuesday. Turns out the barista—this guy named Mateo who always wears those really cool vintage suspenders—was moving away, and everyone came to say goodbye. He's moving to Portland, which I guess makes sense because he's really into, like, artisanal coffee roasting and hiking. I've never been to Portland, but I've heard it rains a lot. Which I kind of like? Rain is nice when you're inside. Though my umbrella is completely broken right now. One of the little metal spokes snapped the last time it was windy, and now it just sort of droops on one side. It looks so sad, like a broken bat wing. I really need to buy a new one, but every time I go to the store, I forget. I went to Target last week specifically for an umbrella and ended up buying three candles and a bath mat instead. The bath mat is super soft, though. It's like stepping on a cloud. \n\nBut yeah, anyway... I still enjoy things. I definitely still feel pleasure from stuff I like.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your low mood is interfering with your daily life?\n\nPatient: Um, interfering? I guess a little bit, yeah. I mean, I'm still doing the basics, like running errands and stuff. But I've definitely been dropping the ball with some of the things people usually count on me for. \n\nLike the volunteer stuff I do, and the fair planning. I'm usually the one who handles all the design follow-through, but lately, I just haven't been as reliable. Which is frustrating because I usually love doing that kind of thing. I spent like three hours last month just picking out the perfect font for the fair flyers. I wanted something that looked rustic but still legible, you know? I ended up going with this one called \"Burlap,\" but then the printer we use—this local place run by this guy who always smells like peppermint—said the font was too thin for their machines. So I had to redo the whole thing in Helvetica, which is just so boring. It's like the vanilla ice cream of fonts. Not that I have anything against vanilla ice cream. Actually, I had some last night with those little chocolate sprinkles. Did you know in some places they call them \"jimmies\"? I read an article about where that word came from once, but I can't remember the exact origin. Something about a guy named Jimmy who ran a candy machine, maybe? Or maybe that's just a myth. Myths are funny like that, how they just become accepted as fact. Like how people say you swallow eight spiders a year in your sleep. That can't possibly be true, right? Spiders don't want to go in a person's mouth. \n\nBut, uh, yeah, I'm still getting through my day-to-day stuff mostly, just not as on top of my creative commitments as I usually am.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your current state is significantly impacting your ability to function in daily life?\n\nPatient: Um, significantly impacting? I mean, I guess it depends on what you mean by significant. I'm still doing the basics, like I said. I go to the grocery store, I do my laundry... mostly. \n\nWell, I did shrink my favorite sweater last week because I wasn't paying attention to the settings. It was this really soft cashmere blend that I got on sale at a boutique downtown. The owner there is this lovely woman who always gives me free samples of these little French soaps. They smell like lavender and honey, which is such a nice combination. I tried making lavender-honey tea once, but I used culinary lavender instead of the kind meant for steeping, and it tasted like I was drinking perfume. It was awful. I ended up pouring the whole pot down the sink, and then my kitchen smelled like a soap factory for three days. Which I guess isn't the worst thing to smell like. Better than that time the garbage disposal broke and my apartment smelled like old broccoli. I had to call the super, and he took forever to fix it because he was busy watching some big soccer match. I don't really get soccer, honestly. There's just so much running back and forth and hardly any scoring. It's kind of like watching a really intense game of ping-pong but on a giant field. \n\nBut, right, my daily life. Um, yeah, I'm getting through the basic errands and stuff, but I'm definitely less reliable with my volunteer work. Like the fair planning and the design follow-through I usually do. People usually count on me for that, and I've just been kind of... dropping the ball lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your current state is making it harder for you to concentrate on things?\n\nPatient: Um, concentrating? No, I don't think so. I haven't really noticed any issues with that. I mean, I can still focus on things just fine. \n\nLike yesterday, I sat down and read this entire article about the history of the Ferris wheel. It was actually super fascinating. Did you know the first one was built for the 1893 World's Fair in Chicago? It was supposed to rival the Eiffel Tower, which is wild to think about. I've always wanted to go to Paris and see the Eiffel Tower, but I've heard the lines are just horrendous. My friend Sarah went last year, and she said she waited for three hours just to get to the second level. And then it started raining, and she didn't have an umbrella. Which reminds me, I still need to buy a new umbrella. I was thinking about getting one of those clear bubble ones. They look so cute, like you're in a little personal greenhouse. Though I guess if it's sunny, you'd literally bake in there like a tomato. I tried growing tomatoes on my balcony once, but the pigeons kept eating them before they even turned red. Pigeons are so bold in the city, aren't they? They just walk right up to you like they own the sidewalk. \n\nBut yeah, no, my concentration is fine. I haven't had any trouble focusing on things.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about when you first started feeling this way and if it's been consistent over the past two months?\n\nPatient: Um, when it started? Yeah, it's been about two months now. It pretty much started right when I decided I needed to completely overhaul my daily routine and how I present myself to the world. I just felt like I needed a fresh start, you know? \n\nI bought all these new planners—the really nice ones with the thick paper where the ink doesn't bleed through. I even got a set of those pastel highlighters because I saw this video of a girl color-coding her entire life, and it just looked so calming. Her desk was perfectly organized, and she had this little succulent in a geometric pot. I tried to keep a succulent once, but I think I overwatered it because it just turned to mush. Everyone says they're the easiest plants to keep alive, but I swear ferns are easier. My grandmother had this massive Boston fern on her porch that she basically ignored, and it thrived. She used to sit out there and knit these really intricate lace doilies. I don't think people use doilies much anymore, do they? They feel very vintage. Kind of like those hard ribbon candies they sell around the holidays. The ones that look pretty but taste like dusty peppermint and always get stuck together in a giant clump in the bowl. \n\nBut, um, yeah, it's been pretty consistent for the last two months, ever since I tried changing up my routine and everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to find pleasure in things you used to enjoy, even if you're technically still doing them?\n\nPatient: Um, finding pleasure in things? Like, I'm still doing my volunteer stuff, even if I'm a little behind on it, and I still like it. \n\nActually, I was just looking at these new fabric swatches for the fair booths yesterday. They have this gorgeous teal color that I think would look amazing against the white tents. Teal is such an underrated color, honestly. It's like blue and green had a perfect baby. I painted my bedroom teal when I was in high school. My dad was so mad because it took like four coats of primer to cover it up when we moved out. We moved to this house that had this huge oak tree in the front yard, and in the fall, it would drop a million acorns. The squirrels were out of control. Like, if you even opened the closet where we kept it, he would run and hide under my bed. Which is funny because he wasn't scared of thunder at all. He would just sleep right through these massive thunderstorms. I love a good thunderstorm, as long as I don't have to drive in it. People completely forget how to drive the second a single drop of rain hits the windshield. It's like their brains just short-circuit. \n\nBut yeah, no, I still enjoy things just fine. I haven't noticed any problem with that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any thoughts of self-harm or suicide?\n\nPatient: Um, thoughts of hurting myself? No, definitely not. I haven't had any thoughts like that at all. I mean, I can't even stand the sight of blood, to be honest. \n\nLike, last week I was making a salad and I accidentally nicked my finger with the paring knife. It wasn't even a deep cut, but it bled a little, and I had to sit down on the kitchen floor for like ten minutes because I felt so lightheaded. I used one of those band-aids with the little cartoon dinosaurs on it. My little cousin left a whole box of them at my apartment last time she visited. She's obsessed with the T-Rex right now, which is so funny because when I was her age, I was completely terrified of dinosaurs. I think it was because my older brother made me watch Jurassic Park when I was way too young. That scene with the Jell-O shaking in the spoon? Still gives me the creeps. But Jell-O is such a weird food anyway, right? It's like a liquid that decided to be a solid but couldn't fully commit. My mom used to make this green Jell-O salad for Thanksgiving with shredded carrots and pineapple in it. Everyone always pretended to like it, but I swear she was the only one who actually ate it. Thanksgiving food is so heavy anyway, you know? By the time you get to dessert, you just want to take a nap on the couch. \n\nBut um, no, to answer your question, I haven't had any thoughts of self-harm or anything like that. I'm totally fine in that department.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about your future most of the time over the past two months?\n\nPatient: Um, hopeless? No, I wouldn't say I feel hopeless about the future. I mean, I'm not really sure what the future looks like right now, but I don't feel bad about it. \n\nActually, speaking of the future, I was just looking at these astrology apps the other day. My friend Sarah is super into all that stuff. She made me download this one that tells you exactly where all the planets are at the exact minute you were born. Apparently, my moon is in Taurus, which she says means I like comfortable things and good food. Which is pretty accurate, honestly. I love a good grilled cheese sandwich. But you have to make it with the right bread, you know? Sourdough is the best because it gets that perfect crunch on the outside. I tried making my own sourdough starter during the pandemic, like everyone else did. I named him Doughy, but I completely forgot to feed him for like a week, and he started smelling like old gym socks. It was so gross. I ended up throwing the whole jar away. Glass jars are so useful, though. I save all my pasta sauce jars to put things in, like buttons and paperclips. I have this one jar that's just full of those little plastic tags they put on bread bags. I don't even know why I save them. Maybe I saw a craft project on Pinterest once and thought I'd do it someday? Pinterest is a black hole. You go on there looking for a recipe for chicken parmesan, and three hours later, you're looking at ideas for a rustic woodland wedding, even though you're not even dating anyone. \n\nBut um, yeah, no, I don't feel hopeless about my future or anything. I'm just kind of taking it day by day right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your low mood is significantly interfering with your ability to function in daily life?\n\nPatient: Um, interfering? I wouldn't say it's significantly interfering, no. I mean, I'm still getting through my basic errands and normal commitments just fine. \n\nLike, I went to the grocery store yesterday to get oat milk. Have you noticed how many types of milk there are now? It's honestly overwhelming. Almond, oat, soy, macadamia... I tried macadamia nut milk once in my coffee and it just tasted like drinking a candle. Speaking of candles, I bought this huge three-wick one the other day that was supposed to smell like \"Autumn Woods,\" but it just smells exactly like cheap men's cologne. Which isn't a bad smell, I guess, but it's really weird when you're trying to eat a bowl of cereal and your kitchen smells like a middle school dance. Middle school was such an awkward time. I had braces and these terrible bangs that I cut myself with kitchen scissors because I thought I could follow a YouTube tutorial. Spoiler alert, I could not. I looked like a medieval peasant for like six months until they grew out. \n\nBut um, as far as functioning goes, I guess I have been less reliable lately in the volunteer and creative roles that people usually count on me for. Like the fair planning and the design follow-through. I was supposed to send over the final layout for the vendor booths on Tuesday, and I just kind of haven't done it yet. Which reminds me, one of the vendors this year is this lady who makes jewelry out of antique spoons. It's actually really cool. She takes the handles and bends them into rings. I bought one last year that had a little rose pattern stamped on it, but I lost it at the beach. The beach is always so much better in theory than in reality, you know? You think it's going to be so relaxing, but then you just end up with sand in your sandwich and a terrible sunburn on the tops of your feet. Sunburned feet are the absolute worst because you can't even put real shoes on for a week. \n\nSo yeah, I'm getting my basic errands done, just kind of dropping the ball a little on the fair designs and stuff."
